53:["$","$L51",null,{"id":"614-the-string-type"}]
5e:T1def,<p>The <dfn variants="is a String,is not a String">String type</dfn> is the set of all ordered sequences of zero or more 16-bit unsigned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> values (“elements”) up to a maximum length of 2<sup>53</sup> - 1 elements. The String type is generally used to represent textual data in a running ECMAScript program, in which case each element in the String is treated as a UTF-16 code unit value. Each element is regarded as occupying a position within the sequence. These positions are indexed with non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref>. The first element (if any) is at index 0, the next element (if any) at index 1, and so on. The length of a String is the number of elements (i.e., 16-bit values) within it. The empty String has length zero and therefore contains no elements.</p>
      <p>ECMAScript operations that do not interpret String contents apply no further semantics. Operations that do interpret String values treat each element as a single UTF-16 code unit. However, ECMAScript does not restrict the value of or relationships between these code units, so operations that further interpret String contents as sequences of Unicode code points encoded in UTF-16 must account for ill-formed subsequences. Such operations apply special treatment to every code unit with a numeric value in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0xD800 to 0xDBFF (defined by the Unicode Standard as a <dfn id="leading-surrogate" variants="leading surrogates">leading surrogate</dfn>, or more formally as a <dfn id="high-surrogate-code-unit" variants="high-surrogate code units">high-surrogate code unit</dfn>) and every code unit with a numeric value in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0xDC00 to 0xDFFF (defined as a <dfn id="trailing-surrogate" variants="trailing surrogates">trailing surrogate</dfn>, or more formally as a <dfn id="low-surrogate-code-unit" variants="low-surrogate code units">low-surrogate code unit</dfn>) using the following rules:</p>
      <ul>
        <li>
          A code unit that is not a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#leading-surrogate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#leading-surrogate">leading surrogate</a></emu-xref> and not a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#trailing-surrogate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#trailing-surrogate">trailing surrogate</a></emu-xref> is interpreted as a code point with the same value.
        </li>
        <li>
          A sequence of two code units, where the first code unit <var>c1</var> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#leading-surrogate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#leading-surrogate">leading surrogate</a></emu-xref> and the second code unit <var>c2</var> a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#trailing-surrogate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#trailing-surrogate">trailing surrogate</a></emu-xref>, is a <dfn id="surrogate-pair" variants="surrogate pairs">surrogate pair</dfn> and is interpreted as a code point with the value (<var>c1</var> - 0xD800) × 0x400 + (<var>c2</var> - 0xDC00) + 0x10000. (See <emu-xref href="#sec-utf16decodesurrogatepair"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-utf16decodesurrogatepair">11.1.3</a></emu-xref>)
        </li>
        <li>
          A code unit that is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#leading-surrogate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#leading-surrogate">leading surrogate</a></emu-xref> or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#trailing-surrogate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#trailing-surrogate">trailing surrogate</a></emu-xref>, but is not part of a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#surrogate-pair"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#surrogate-pair">surrogate pair</a></emu-xref>, is interpreted as a code point with the same value.
        </li>
      </ul>
      <p>The function <code>String.prototype.normalize</code> (see <emu-xref href="#sec-string.prototype.normalize"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-string.prototype.normalize">22.1.3.15</a></emu-xref>) can be used to explicitly normalize a String value. <code>String.prototype.localeCompare</code> (see <emu-xref href="#sec-string.prototype.localecompare"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-string.prototype.localecompare">22.1.3.12</a></emu-xref>) internally normalizes String values, but no other operations implicitly normalize the strings upon which they operate. Operation results are not language- and/or locale-sensitive unless stated otherwise.</p>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The rationale behind this design was to keep the implementation of Strings as simple and high-performing as possible. If <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-source-text"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-source-text">ECMAScript source text</a></emu-xref> is in Normalized Form C, string literals are guaranteed to also be normalized, as long as they do not contain any Unicode escape sequences.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <p>In this specification, the phrase “the <dfn id="string-concatenation">string-concatenation</dfn> of <var>A</var>, <var>B</var>, ...” (where each argument is a String value, a code unit, or a sequence of code units) denotes the String value whose sequence of code units is the concatenation of the code units (in order) of each of the arguments (in order).</p>
      <p>The phrase “the <dfn id="substring">substring</dfn> of <var>string</var> from <var>inclusiveStart</var> to <var>exclusiveEnd</var>” (where <var>string</var> is a String value or a sequence of code units and <var>inclusiveStart</var> and <var>exclusiveEnd</var> are <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref>) denotes the String value consisting of the consecutive code units of <var>string</var> beginning at index <var>inclusiveStart</var> and ending immediately before index <var>exclusiveEnd</var> (which is the empty String when <var>inclusiveStart</var> = <var>exclusiveEnd</var>). If the "to" suffix is omitted, the length of <var>string</var> is used as the value of <var>exclusiveEnd</var>.</p>
      <p>
        The phrase “<dfn id="ASCII-word-characters">the ASCII word characters</dfn>” denotes the following String value, which consists solely of every letter and number in the Unicode Basic Latin block along with U+005F (LOW LINE):<br>
        <emu-val>"ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789_"</emu-val>.<br>
        For historical reasons, it has significance to various algorithms.
      </p>

5f:T93f,<p>The abstract operation StringIndexOf takes arguments <var>string</var> (a String), <var>searchValue</var> (a String), and <var>fromIndex</var> (a non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref>) and returns a non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> or <emu-const>not-found</emu-const>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>length</var> be the length of <var>string</var>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>searchValue</var> is the empty String and <var>fromIndex</var> ≤ <var>length</var>, return <var>fromIndex</var>.</li><li>Let <var>searchLength</var> be the length of <var>searchValue</var>.</li><li>For each <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> <var>i</var> such that <var>fromIndex</var> ≤ <var>i</var> ≤ <var>length</var> - <var>searchLength</var>, in ascending order, do<ol><li>Let <var>candidate</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#substring"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#substring">substring</a></emu-xref> of <var>string</var> from <var>i</var> to <var>i</var> + <var>searchLength</var>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>candidate</var> is <var>searchValue</var>, return <var>i</var>.</li></ol></li><li class="exit">Return <emu-const>not-found</emu-const>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>If <var>searchValue</var> is the empty String and <var>fromIndex</var> ≤ the length of <var>string</var>, this algorithm returns <var>fromIndex</var>. The empty String is effectively found at every position within a string, including after the last code unit.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>This algorithm always returns <emu-const>not-found</emu-const> if <var>fromIndex</var> + the length of <var>searchValue</var> > the length of <var>string</var>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

60:T7fb,<p>The abstract operation StringLastIndexOf takes arguments <var>string</var> (a String), <var>searchValue</var> (a String), and <var>fromIndex</var> (a non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref>) and returns a non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> or <emu-const>not-found</emu-const>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>length</var> be the length of <var>string</var>.</li><li>Let <var>searchLength</var> be the length of <var>searchValue</var>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>fromIndex</var> + <var>searchLength</var> ≤ <var>length</var>.</li><li>For each <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> <var>i</var> such that 0 ≤ <var>i</var> ≤ <var>fromIndex</var>, in descending order, do<ol><li>Let <var>candidate</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#substring"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#substring">substring</a></emu-xref> of <var>string</var> from <var>i</var> to <var>i</var> + <var>searchLength</var>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>candidate</var> is <var>searchValue</var>, return <var>i</var>.</li></ol></li><li class="exit">Return <emu-const>not-found</emu-const>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>If <var>searchValue</var> is the empty String, this algorithm returns <var>fromIndex</var>. The empty String is effectively found at every position within a string, including after the last code unit.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

61:T28b8,<p>Well-known symbols are built-in Symbol values that are explicitly referenced by algorithms of this specification. They are typically used as the keys of properties whose values serve as extension points of a specification algorithm. Unless otherwise specified, well-known symbols values are shared by all <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm">realms</a></emu-xref> (<emu-xref href="#sec-code-realms"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-code-realms">9.3</a></emu-xref>).</p>
        <p>Within this specification a well-known symbol is referred to using the standard <emu-xref href="#sec-well-known-intrinsic-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-well-known-intrinsic-objects">intrinsic notation</a></emu-xref> where the intrinsic is one of the values listed in <emu-xref href="#table-well-known-symbols"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-well-known-symbols">Table 1</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">Previous editions of this specification used a notation of the form @@name, where the current edition would use <code>%Symbol.name%</code>. In particular, the following names were used: @@asyncIterator, @@hasInstance, @@isConcatSpreadable, @@<emu-not-ref>iterator</emu-not-ref>, @@match, @@matchAll, @@replace, @@search, @@species, @@split, @@toPrimitive, @@toStringTag, and @@unscopables.</div></emu-note>

                </th>

Called by the semantics of the <code>for</code>-<code>await</code>-<code>of</code> statement.
              </td>

              </td>

              </td>

Called by the semantics of the for-of statement.
              </td>
            </tr>

              <td>

              </td>
            </tr>

              </td>

Called by the <emu-xref href="#sec-string.prototype.matchall"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-string.prototype.matchall"><code>String.prototype.matchAll</code></a></emu-xref> method.

              </td>

Called by the <emu-xref href="#sec-string.prototype.search"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/text-processing#sec-string.prototype.search"><code>String.prototype.search</code></a></emu-xref> method.
              </td>

              </td>
            </tr>

              </td>
            </tr>

              </td>

              </td>

              </td>

              </td>

      57:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-ecmascript-language-types-symbol-type","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"615-the-symbol-type","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.1.5"}]," The Symbol Type"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"615-the-symbol-type"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"<p>The <dfn variants=\"is a Symbol,is not a Symbol\">Symbol type</dfn> is the set of all non-String values that may be used as the key of an Object property (<emu-xref href=\"#sec-object-type\"><a href=\"/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type\">6.1.7</a></emu-xref>).</p>\n      <p>Each Symbol is unique and immutable.</p>\n      <p>Each Symbol has an immutable <var class=\"field\">[[Description]]</var> internal slot whose value is either a String or <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>.</p>\n\n      "}}],["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-well-known-symbols","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"6151-well-known-symbols","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.1.5.1"}]," Well-Known Symbols"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"6151-well-known-symbols"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$61"}}]]}]]}]
62:T59fb,<p>ECMAScript has two built-in numeric types: Number and BigInt. The following <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> are defined over these numeric types. The “Result” column shows the return type, along with an indication if it is possible for some invocations of the operation to return an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">abrupt completion</a></emu-xref>.</p>

      <p>Because the numeric types are in general not convertible without loss of precision or truncation, the ECMAScript language provides no implicit conversion among these types. Programmers must explicitly call <code>Number</code> and <code>BigInt</code> functions to convert among types when calling a function which requires another type.</p>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The first and subsequent editions of ECMAScript have provided, for certain operators, implicit numeric conversions that could lose precision or <emu-xref aoid="truncate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#eqn-truncate">truncate</a></emu-xref>. These legacy implicit conversions are maintained for backward compatibility, but not provided for BigInt in order to minimize opportunity for programmer error, and to leave open the option of generalized <em>value types</em> in a future edition.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

      58:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-numeric-types","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"616-numeric-types","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.1.6"}]," Numeric Types"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"616-numeric-types"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$62"}}],"$L63","$L64"]}]
65:T1d1c,<p>Each instance of the <dfn variants="is an Object,is not an Object">Object type</dfn>, also referred to simply as “an Object”, represents a collection of properties. Each property is either a data property, or an accessor property:</p>
      <ul>
        <li>
          A <dfn variants="data properties">data property</dfn> associates a key value with an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref> and a set of Boolean attributes.
        </li>
        <li>
          An <dfn variants="accessor properties">accessor property</dfn> associates a key value with one or two accessor functions, and a set of Boolean attributes. The accessor functions are used to store or retrieve an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref> that is associated with the property.
        </li>
      </ul>
      <p>The properties of an object are uniquely identified using <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">property keys</a></emu-xref>. A <dfn id="property-key" variants="property keys" oldids="sec-ispropertykey">property key</dfn> is either a String or a Symbol. All Strings and Symbols, including the empty String, are valid as <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">property keys</a></emu-xref>. A <dfn id="property-name" variants="property names">property name</dfn> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">property key</a></emu-xref> that <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-string-type">is a String</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <p>An <dfn id="integer-index" variants="integer indices,integer-indexed">integer index</dfn> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-name"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-name">property name</a></emu-xref> <var>n</var> such that <emu-xref aoid="CanonicalNumericIndexString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-canonicalnumericindexstring">CanonicalNumericIndexString</a></emu-xref>(<var>n</var>) returns an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> to <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(2<sup>53</sup> - 1). An <dfn id="array-index" variants="array indices">array index</dfn> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index">integer index</a></emu-xref> <var>n</var> such that <emu-xref aoid="CanonicalNumericIndexString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-canonicalnumericindexstring">CanonicalNumericIndexString</a></emu-xref>(<var>n</var>) returns an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integral-number">integral Number</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> to <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(2<sup>32</sup> - 2).</p>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Every non-negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#safe-integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#safe-integer">safe integer</a></emu-xref> has a corresponding <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index">integer index</a></emu-xref>. Every 32-bit unsigned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> except <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>32</sup> - 1</emu-eqn> has a corresponding <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#array-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#array-index">array index</a></emu-xref>. <emu-val>"-0"</emu-val> is neither an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#integer-index">integer index</a></emu-xref> nor an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#array-index"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#array-index">array index</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <p><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">Property keys</a></emu-xref> are used to access properties and their values. There are two kinds of access for properties: <em>get</em> and <em>set</em>, corresponding to value retrieval and assignment, respectively. The properties accessible via get and set access includes both <em>own properties</em> that are a direct part of an object and <em>inherited properties</em> which are provided by another associated object via a property inheritance relationship. Inherited properties may be either own or inherited properties of the associated object. Each own property of an object must each have a key value that is distinct from the key values of the other own properties of that object.</p>
      <p>All objects are logically collections of properties, but there are multiple forms of objects that differ in their semantics for accessing and manipulating their properties. Please see <emu-xref href="#sec-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots">6.1.7.2</a></emu-xref> for definitions of the multiple forms of objects.</p>
      <p>In addition, some objects are callable; these are referred to as functions or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object">function objects</a></emu-xref> and are described further below. All functions in ECMAScript are members of the Object type.</p>

      59:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-object-type","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"617-the-object-type","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.1.7"}]," The Object Type"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"617-the-object-type"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$65"}}],"$L66","$L67","$L68","$L69"]}]
6a:T95b,<p>A specification type corresponds to meta-values that are used within algorithms to describe the semantics of ECMAScript language constructs and <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language types</a></emu-xref>. The specification types include <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-reference-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-reference-record-specification-type">Reference Record</a></emu-xref>, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref>, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref>, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type">Property Descriptor</a></emu-xref>, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records">Environment Record</a></emu-xref>, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-abstract-closure">Abstract Closure</a></emu-xref>, and <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-data-blocks"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-data-blocks">Data Block</a></emu-xref>. Specification type values are specification artefacts that do not necessarily correspond to any specific entity within an ECMAScript implementation. Specification type values may be used to describe intermediate results of ECMAScript expression evaluation but such values cannot be stored as properties of objects or values of ECMAScript language variables.</p>

    5a:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-ecmascript-specification-types","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"62-ecmascript-specification-types","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2"}]," ECMAScript Specification Types"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"62-ecmascript-specification-types"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$6a"}}],["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-enum-specification-type","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"621-the-enum-specification-type","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.1"}]," The Enum Specification Type"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"621-the-enum-specification-type"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"<p><dfn variants=\"enum,enums\">Enums</dfn> are values which are internal to the specification and not directly observable from ECMAScript code. Enums are denoted in upper kebab case using a <emu-const>sans-serif</emu-const> typeface. For instance, a <emu-xref href=\"/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type\"><a href=\"/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type\">Completion Record</a></emu-xref>'s <var class=\"field\">[[Type]]</var> field takes on values like <emu-const>normal</emu-const>, <emu-const>return</emu-const>, or <emu-const>throw</emu-const>. Enums have no characteristics other than their name. The name of an enum serves no purpose other than to distinguish it from other enums, and implies nothing about its usage or meaning in context.</p>\n    "}}]]}],"$L6b","$L6c","$L6d","$L6e","$L6f","$L70","$L71","$L72","$L73","$L74","$L75","$L76"]}]
5d:null
77:T28fd,<p>The <dfn variants="is a Number,is not a Number">Number type</dfn> has exactly 18,437,736,874,454,810,627 (that is, <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>64</sup> - 2<sup>53</sup> + 3</emu-eqn>) values, representing the double-precision floating point <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography">IEEE 754-2019</a></emu-xref> binary64 values as specified in the IEEE Standard for Binary Floating-Point Arithmetic, except that the 9,007,199,254,740,990 (that is, <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>53</sup> - 2</emu-eqn>) distinct NaN values of the IEEE Standard are represented in ECMAScript as a single special <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> value. (Note that the <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> value is produced by the program expression <code>NaN</code>.) In some implementations, external code might be able to detect a difference between various NaN values, but such behaviour is <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref>; to ECMAScript code, all <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> values are indistinguishable from each other.</p>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>The bit pattern that might be observed in an ArrayBuffer (see <emu-xref href="#sec-arraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-arraybuffer-objects">25.1</a></emu-xref>) or a SharedArrayBuffer (see <emu-xref href="#sec-sharedarraybuffer-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/structured-data#sec-sharedarraybuffer-objects">25.2</a></emu-xref>) after a Number value has been stored into it is not necessarily the same as the internal representation of that Number value used by the ECMAScript implementation.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <p>There are two other special values, called <emu-val>positive Infinity</emu-val> and <emu-val>negative Infinity</emu-val>. For brevity, these values are also referred to for expository purposes by the symbols <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> and <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, respectively. (Note that these two infinite Number values are produced by the program expressions <code>+Infinity</code> (or simply <code>Infinity</code>) and <code>-Infinity</code>.)</p>
        <p>The other 18,437,736,874,454,810,624 (that is, <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>64</sup> - 2<sup>53</sup></emu-eqn>) values are called the <dfn id="finite">finite</dfn> numbers. Half of these are positive numbers and half are negative numbers; for every <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> positive Number value there is a corresponding negative value having the same magnitude.</p>
        <p>Note that there is both a <emu-val>positive zero</emu-val> and a <emu-val>negative zero</emu-val>. For brevity, these values are also referred to for expository purposes by the symbols <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> and <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, respectively. (Note that these two different zero Number values are produced by the program expressions <code>+0</code> (or simply <code>0</code>) and <code>-0</code>.)</p>
        <p>The 18,437,736,874,454,810,622 (that is, <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>64</sup> - 2<sup>53</sup> - 2</emu-eqn>) <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> non-zero values are of two kinds:</p>
        <p>18,428,729,675,200,069,632 (that is, <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>64</sup> - 2<sup>54</sup></emu-eqn>) of them are normalized, having the form</p>
        <div class="math-display">
          <var>s</var> × <var>m</var> × 2<sup><var>e</var></sup>
        </div>
        <p>where <var>s</var> is 1 or -1, <var>m</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#interval">interval</a></emu-xref> from 2<sup>52</sup> (inclusive) to 2<sup>53</sup> (exclusive), and <var>e</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from -1074 to 971.</p>
        <p>The remaining 9,007,199,254,740,990 (that is, <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>53</sup> - 2</emu-eqn>) values are denormalized, having the form</p>
        <div class="math-display">
          <var>s</var> × <var>m</var> × 2<sup><var>e</var></sup>
        </div>
        <p>where <var>s</var> is 1 or -1, <var>m</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#interval">interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 (exclusive) to 2<sup>52</sup> (exclusive), and <var>e</var> is -1074.</p>
        <p>Note that all the positive and negative <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref> whose magnitude is no greater than 2<sup>53</sup> are representable in the Number type. The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> 0 has two representations in the Number type: <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> and <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</p>
        <p>A <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> number has an <em>odd significand</em> if it is non-zero and the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> <var>m</var> used to express it (in one of the two forms shown above) is odd. Otherwise, it has an <em>even significand</em>.</p>
        <p>In this specification, the phrase “the <dfn id="number-value-for" oldids="number-value">Number value for</dfn> <var>x</var>” where <var>x</var> represents an exact real mathematical quantity (which might even be an irrational number such as π) means a Number value chosen in the following manner. Consider the set of all <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> values of the Number type, with <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> removed and with two additional values added to it that are not representable in the Number type, namely 2<sup>1024</sup> (which is <emu-eqn class="inline">+1 × 2<sup>53</sup> × 2<sup>971</sup></emu-eqn>) and <emu-eqn class="inline">-2<sup>1024</sup></emu-eqn> (which is <emu-eqn class="inline">-1 × 2<sup>53</sup> × 2<sup>971</sup></emu-eqn>). Choose the member of this set that is closest in value to <var>x</var>. If two values of the set are equally close, then the one with an even significand is chosen; for this purpose, the two extra values 2<sup>1024</sup> and <emu-eqn class="inline">-2<sup>1024</sup></emu-eqn> are considered to have even significands. Finally, if 2<sup>1024</sup> was chosen, replace it with <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>; if <emu-eqn class="inline">-2<sup>1024</sup></emu-eqn> was chosen, replace it with <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>; if <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> was chosen, replace it with <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> if and only if <var>x</var> &lt; 0; any other chosen value is used unchanged. The result is the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#number-value-for"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#number-value-for">Number value for</a></emu-xref> <var>x</var>. (This procedure corresponds exactly to the behaviour of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography">IEEE 754-2019</a></emu-xref> roundTiesToEven mode.)</p>
        <p>The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#number-value-for"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#number-value-for">Number value for</a></emu-xref> +∞ is <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, and the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#number-value-for"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#number-value-for">Number value for</a></emu-xref> -∞ is <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</p>
        <p>Some ECMAScript operators deal only with <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref> in specific ranges such as the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from <emu-eqn class="inline">-2<sup>31</sup></emu-eqn> to <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>31</sup> - 1</emu-eqn> or the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to <emu-eqn class="inline">2<sup>16</sup> - 1</emu-eqn>. These operators accept any value of the Number type but first convert each such value to an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> value in the expected range. See the descriptions of the numeric conversion operations in <emu-xref href="#sec-type-conversion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-type-conversion">7.1</a></emu-xref>.</p>

8c:T48c,<p>The abstract operation BigInt::exponentiate takes arguments <var>base</var> (a BigInt) and <var>exponent</var> (a BigInt) and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion containing</a></emu-xref> a BigInt or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">throw completion</a></emu-xref>.           <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">If <var>exponent</var> &lt; <emu-val>0</emu-val><sub>ℤ</sub>, throw a <emu-val>RangeError</emu-val> exception.</li><li class="exit">If <var>base</var> = <emu-val>0</emu-val><sub>ℤ</sub> and <var>exponent</var> = <emu-val>0</emu-val><sub>ℤ</sub>, return <emu-val>1</emu-val><sub>ℤ</sub>.</li><li class="exit">Return <var>base</var> raised to the power <var>exponent</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>

Unless specified explicitly, the initial value of each attribute is its Default Value.</p>

The effect of a property's <var class="field">[[Set]]</var> internal method may, but is not required to, have an effect on the value returned by subsequent calls to the property's <var class="field">[[Get]]</var> internal method.

              </td>

              </td>
            </tr>
          </table>
        </emu-table>
      66:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-property-attributes","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"6171-property-attributes","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.1.7.1"}]," Property Attributes"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"6171-property-attributes"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$9f"}}]]}]
a0:T5f6b,<p>The actual semantics of objects, in ECMAScript, are specified via algorithms called <em>internal methods</em>. Each object in an ECMAScript engine is associated with a set of internal methods that defines its runtime behaviour. These internal methods are not part of the ECMAScript language. They are defined by this specification purely for expository purposes. However, each object within an implementation of ECMAScript must behave as specified by the internal methods associated with it. The exact manner in which this is accomplished is determined by the implementation.</p>
        <p>Internal method names are polymorphic. This means that different object values may perform different algorithms when a common internal method name is invoked upon them. That actual object upon which an internal method is invoked is the “target” of the invocation. If, at runtime, the implementation of an algorithm attempts to use an internal method of an object that the object does not support, a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception is thrown.</p>
        <p>Internal slots correspond to internal state that is associated with objects, Symbols, or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-private-names"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-private-names">Private Names</a></emu-xref> and used by various ECMAScript specification algorithms. Internal slots are not object properties and they are not inherited. Depending upon the specific internal slot specification, such state may consist of values of any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language type</a></emu-xref> or of specific ECMAScript specification type values. Unless explicitly specified otherwise, internal slots are allocated as part of the process of creating an object, Symbol, or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-private-names"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-private-names">Private Name</a></emu-xref> and may not be dynamically added. Unless specified otherwise, the initial value of an internal slot is the value <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>. Various algorithms within this specification create values that have internal slots. However, the ECMAScript language provides no direct way to manipulate internal slots.</p>
        <p>All objects have an internal slot named <var class="field">[[PrivateElements]]</var>, which is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-privateelement-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-privateelement-specification-type">PrivateElements</a></emu-xref>. This <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> represents the values of the private fields, methods, and accessors for the object. Initially, it is an empty <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>Internal methods and internal slots are identified within this specification using names enclosed in double square brackets [[ ]].</p>
        <p><emu-xref href="#table-essential-internal-methods"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-essential-internal-methods">Table 4</a></emu-xref> summarizes the <em>essential internal methods</em> used by this specification that are applicable to all objects created or manipulated by ECMAScript code. Every object must have algorithms for all of the essential internal methods. However, all objects do not necessarily use the same algorithms for those methods.</p>
        <p>An <dfn id="ordinary-object" variants="ordinary objects">ordinary object</dfn> is an object that satisfies all of the following criteria:</p>
        <ul>
          <li>
            For the internal methods listed in <emu-xref href="#table-essential-internal-methods"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-essential-internal-methods">Table 4</a></emu-xref>, the object uses those defined in <emu-xref href="#sec-ordinary-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-ordinary-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots">10.1</a></emu-xref>.
          </li>
          <li>
            If the object has a <var class="field">[[Call]]</var> internal method, it uses either the one defined in <emu-xref href="#sec-ecmascript-function-objects-call-thisargument-argumentslist"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-ecmascript-function-objects-call-thisargument-argumentslist">10.2.1</a></emu-xref> or the one defined in <emu-xref href="#sec-built-in-function-objects-call-thisargument-argumentslist"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-built-in-function-objects-call-thisargument-argumentslist">10.3.1</a></emu-xref>.
          </li>
          <li>
            If the object has a <var class="field">[[Construct]]</var> internal method, it uses either the one defined in <emu-xref href="#sec-ecmascript-function-objects-construct-argumentslist-newtarget"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-ecmascript-function-objects-construct-argumentslist-newtarget">10.2.2</a></emu-xref> or the one defined in <emu-xref href="#sec-built-in-function-objects-construct-argumentslist-newtarget"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-built-in-function-objects-construct-argumentslist-newtarget">10.3.2</a></emu-xref>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <p>An <dfn id="exotic-object" variants="exotic objects">exotic object</dfn> is an object that is not an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary object</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>This specification recognizes different kinds of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic objects</a></emu-xref> by those objects' internal methods. An object that is behaviourally equivalent to a particular kind of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic object</a></emu-xref> (such as an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#array-exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#array-exotic-object">Array exotic object</a></emu-xref> or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#bound-function-exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#bound-function-exotic-object">bound function exotic object</a></emu-xref>), but does not have the same collection of internal methods specified for that kind, is not recognized as that kind of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic object</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>The “Signature” column of <emu-xref href="#table-essential-internal-methods"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-essential-internal-methods">Table 4</a></emu-xref> and other similar tables describes the invocation pattern for each internal method. The invocation pattern always includes a parenthesized list of descriptive parameter names. If a parameter name is the same as an ECMAScript type name then the name describes the required type of the parameter value. If an internal method explicitly returns a value, its parameter list is followed by the symbol “→” and the type name of the returned value. The type names used in signatures refer to the types defined in clause <emu-xref href="#sec-ecmascript-data-types-and-values"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-data-types-and-values">6</a></emu-xref> augmented by the following additional names. “<em>any</em>” means the value may be any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language type</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>In addition to its parameters, an internal method always has access to the object that is the target of the method invocation.</p>
        <p>An internal method implicitly returns a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref>, either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion</a></emu-xref> that wraps a value of the return type shown in its invocation pattern, or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">throw completion</a></emu-xref>.</p>
