        <p><emu-xref href="#table-additional-essential-internal-methods-of-function-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-additional-essential-internal-methods-of-function-objects">Table 5</a></emu-xref> summarizes additional essential internal methods that are supported by objects that may be called as functions. A <dfn id="function-object" variants="function objects">function object</dfn> is an object that supports the <var class="field">[[Call]]</var> internal method. A <dfn id="constructor" variants="constructors">constructor</dfn> is an object that supports the <var class="field">[[Construct]]</var> internal method. Every object that supports <var class="field">[[Construct]]</var> must support <var class="field">[[Call]]</var>; that is, every <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> must be a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object">function object</a></emu-xref>. Therefore, a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> may also be referred to as a <em><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> function</em> or <em><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object">function object</a></emu-xref></em>.</p>
        <emu-table id="table-additional-essential-internal-methods-of-function-objects" caption="Additional Essential Internal Methods of Function Objects" oldids="table-6" data-num="5">
          <table>
            <thead>
              <tr>
                <th>
                  Internal Method
                </th>
                <th>
                  Signature
                </th>
                <th>
                  Description
                </th>
              </tr>
            </thead>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <var class="field">[[Call]]</var>
              </td>
              <td>
                (<em>any</em>, a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of <em>any</em>) <b>→</b> <em>any</em>
              </td>
              <td>
                Executes code associated with this object. Invoked via a function call expression. The arguments to the internal method are a <emu-val>this</emu-val> value and a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> whose elements are the arguments passed to the function by a call expression. Objects that implement this internal method are <em>callable</em>.
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <var class="field">[[Construct]]</var>
              </td>
              <td>
                (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of <em>any</em>, Object) <b>→</b> Object
              </td>
              <td>
                Creates an object. Invoked via the <code>new</code> operator or a <code>super</code> call. The first argument to the internal method is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> whose elements are the arguments of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> invocation or the <code>super</code> call. The second argument is the object to which the <code>new</code> operator was initially applied. Objects that implement this internal method are called <em><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructors</a></emu-xref></em>. A <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object">function object</a></emu-xref> is not necessarily a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> and such non-<emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#constructor">constructor</a></emu-xref> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#function-object">function objects</a></emu-xref> do not have a <var class="field">[[Construct]]</var> internal method.
              </td>
            </tr>
          </table>
        </emu-table>
        <p>The semantics of the essential internal methods for <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary objects</a></emu-xref> and standard <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic objects</a></emu-xref> are specified in clause <emu-xref href="#sec-ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours">10</a></emu-xref>. If any specified use of an internal method of an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic object</a></emu-xref> is not supported by an implementation, that usage must throw a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception when attempted.</p>
      67:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"6172-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.1.7.2"}]," Object Internal Methods and Internal Slots"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"6172-object-internal-methods-and-internal-slots"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$a0"}}]]}]
a1:T4dcc,<p>The Internal Methods of Objects of an ECMAScript engine must conform to the list of invariants specified below. Ordinary ECMAScript Objects as well as all standard <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic objects</a></emu-xref> in this specification maintain these invariants. ECMAScript Proxy objects maintain these invariants by means of runtime checks on the result of traps invoked on the <var class="field">[[ProxyHandler]]</var> object.</p>
        <p>Any implementation provided <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic objects</a></emu-xref> must also maintain these invariants for those objects. Violation of these invariants may cause ECMAScript code to have unpredictable behaviour and create security issues. However, violation of these invariants must never compromise the memory safety of an implementation.</p>
        <p>An implementation must not allow these invariants to be circumvented in any manner such as by providing alternative interfaces that implement the functionality of the essential internal methods without enforcing their invariants.</p>
        <h2>Definitions:</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The <em>target</em> of an internal method is the object upon which the internal method is called.
          </li>
          <li>
            A target is <em>non-extensible</em> if it has been observed to return <emu-val>false</emu-val> from its <var class="field">[[IsExtensible]]</var> internal method, or <emu-val>true</emu-val> from its <var class="field">[[PreventExtensions]]</var> internal method.
          </li>
          <li>
            A <em>non-existent</em> property is a property that does not exist as an own property on a non-extensible target.
          </li>
          <li>
            All references to <em><emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref></em> are according to the definition of the <emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref> algorithm.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2>Return value:</h2>
        <p>The value returned by any internal method must be a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref> with either:</p>
        <ul>
          <li><var class="field">[[Type]]</var> = <emu-const>normal</emu-const>, <var class="field">[[Target]]</var> = <emu-const>empty</emu-const>, and <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> = a value of the “normal return type” shown below for that internal method, or</li>
          <li><var class="field">[[Type]]</var> = <emu-const>throw</emu-const>, <var class="field">[[Target]]</var> = <emu-const>empty</emu-const>, and <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> = any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref>.</li>
        </ul>
        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>An internal method must not return a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">continue completion</a></emu-xref>, a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">break completion</a></emu-xref>, or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">return completion</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <h2><var class="field">[[GetPrototypeOf]]</var> ( )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is either Object or Null.
          </li>
          <li>
            If target is non-extensible, and <var class="field">[[GetPrototypeOf]]</var> returns a value <var>proto</var>, then any future calls to <var class="field">[[GetPrototypeOf]]</var> should return the <emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref> as <var>proto</var>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>An object's prototype chain should have <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> length (that is, starting from any object, recursively applying the <var class="field">[[GetPrototypeOf]]</var> internal method to its result should eventually lead to the value <emu-val>null</emu-val>). However, this requirement is not enforceable as an object level invariant if the prototype chain includes any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic objects</a></emu-xref> that do not use the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary object</a></emu-xref> definition of <var class="field">[[GetPrototypeOf]]</var>. Such a circular prototype chain may result in infinite loops when accessing object properties.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <h2><var class="field">[[SetPrototypeOf]]</var> ( <var>proto</var> )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is Boolean.
          </li>
          <li>
            If target is non-extensible, <var class="field">[[SetPrototypeOf]]</var> must return <emu-val>false</emu-val>, unless <var>proto</var> is the <emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref> as the target's observed <var class="field">[[GetPrototypeOf]]</var> value.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[IsExtensible]]</var> ( )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is Boolean.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var class="field">[[IsExtensible]]</var> returns <emu-val>false</emu-val>, all future calls to <var class="field">[[IsExtensible]]</var> on the target must return <emu-val>false</emu-val>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[PreventExtensions]]</var> ( )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is Boolean.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var class="field">[[PreventExtensions]]</var> returns <emu-val>true</emu-val>, all future calls to <var class="field">[[IsExtensible]]</var> on the target must return <emu-val>false</emu-val> and the target is now considered non-extensible.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[GetOwnProperty]]</var> ( <var>propertyKey</var> )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is either <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type">Property Descriptor</a></emu-xref> or Undefined.
          </li>
          <li>
            If the return value is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type">Property Descriptor</a></emu-xref>, it must be a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type">fully populated Property Descriptor</a></emu-xref>.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var>propertyKey</var> is described as a non-configurable, non-writable own <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data property</a></emu-xref>, all future calls to <var class="field">[[GetOwnProperty]]</var> ( <var>propertyKey</var> ) must return <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type">Property Descriptor</a></emu-xref> whose <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> is <emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref> as <var>propertyKey</var>'s <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> attribute.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var>propertyKey</var>'s attributes other than <var class="field">[[Writable]]</var> and <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> may change over time, or if the property might be deleted, then <var>propertyKey</var>'s <var class="field">[[Configurable]]</var> attribute must be <emu-val>true</emu-val>.
          </li>
          <li>
            If the <var class="field">[[Writable]]</var> attribute may change from <emu-val>false</emu-val> to <emu-val>true</emu-val>, then the <var class="field">[[Configurable]]</var> attribute must be <emu-val>true</emu-val>.
          </li>
          <li>
            If the target is non-extensible and <var>propertyKey</var> is non-existent, then all future calls to <var class="field">[[GetOwnProperty]]</var> (<var>propertyKey</var>) on the target must describe <var>propertyKey</var> as non-existent (i.e. <var class="field">[[GetOwnProperty]]</var> (<var>propertyKey</var>) must return <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>).
          </li>
        </ul>
        <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>As a consequence of the third invariant, if a property is described as a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data property</a></emu-xref> and it may return different values over time, then either or both of the <var class="field">[[Writable]]</var> and <var class="field">[[Configurable]]</var> attributes must be <emu-val>true</emu-val> even if no mechanism to change the value is exposed via the other essential internal methods.</p>
        </div></emu-note>
        <h2><var class="field">[[DefineOwnProperty]]</var> ( <var>propertyKey</var>, <var>propertyDesc</var> )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is Boolean.
          </li>
          <li>
            <var class="field">[[DefineOwnProperty]]</var> must return <emu-val>false</emu-val> if <var>propertyKey</var> has previously been observed as a non-configurable own property of the target, unless either:
            <ol>
              <li>
                <var>propertyKey</var> is a writable <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data property</a></emu-xref>. A non-configurable writable <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data property</a></emu-xref> can be changed into a non-configurable non-writable <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data property</a></emu-xref>.
              </li>
              <li>
                All attributes of <var>propertyDesc</var> are the <emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref> as <var>propertyKey</var>'s attributes.
              </li>
            </ol>
          </li>
          <li>
            <var class="field">[[DefineOwnProperty]]</var> (<var>propertyKey</var>, <var>propertyDesc</var>) must return <emu-val>false</emu-val> if target is non-extensible and <var>propertyKey</var> is a non-existent own property. That is, a non-extensible target object cannot be extended with new properties.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[HasProperty]]</var> ( <var>propertyKey</var> )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is Boolean.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var>propertyKey</var> was previously observed as a non-configurable own data or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">accessor property</a></emu-xref> of the target, <var class="field">[[HasProperty]]</var> must return <emu-val>true</emu-val>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[Get]]</var> ( <var>propertyKey</var>, <var>receiver</var> )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language type</a></emu-xref>.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var>propertyKey</var> was previously observed as a non-configurable, non-writable own <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data property</a></emu-xref> of the target with value <var>value</var>, then <var class="field">[[Get]]</var> must return the <emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref> as <var>value</var>.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var>propertyKey</var> was previously observed as a non-configurable own <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">accessor property</a></emu-xref> of the target whose <var class="field">[[Get]]</var> attribute is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, the <var class="field">[[Get]]</var> operation must return <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[Set]]</var> ( <var>propertyKey</var>, <var>value</var>, <var>receiver</var> )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is Boolean.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var>propertyKey</var> was previously observed as a non-configurable, non-writable own <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data property</a></emu-xref> of the target, then <var class="field">[[Set]]</var> must return <emu-val>false</emu-val> unless <var>value</var> is the <emu-xref aoid="SameValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-samevalue">SameValue</a></emu-xref> as <var>propertyKey</var>'s <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> attribute.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var>propertyKey</var> was previously observed as a non-configurable own <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">accessor property</a></emu-xref> of the target whose <var class="field">[[Set]]</var> attribute is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, the <var class="field">[[Set]]</var> operation must return <emu-val>false</emu-val>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[Delete]]</var> ( <var>propertyKey</var> )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is Boolean.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <var>propertyKey</var> was previously observed as a non-configurable own data or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">accessor property</a></emu-xref> of the target, <var class="field">[[Delete]]</var> must return <emu-val>false</emu-val>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[OwnPropertyKeys]]</var> ( )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref>.
          </li>
          <li>
            The returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> must not contain any duplicate entries.
          </li>
          <li>
            Each element of the returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> must be a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">property key</a></emu-xref>.
          </li>
          <li>
            The returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> must contain at least the keys of all non-configurable own properties that have previously been observed.
          </li>
          <li>
            If the target is non-extensible, the returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> must contain only the keys of all own properties of the target that are observable using <var class="field">[[GetOwnProperty]]</var>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[Call]]</var> ( )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language type</a></emu-xref>.
          </li>
        </ul>
        <h2><var class="field">[[Construct]]</var> ( )</h2>
        <ul>
          <li>
            The normal return type is Object.
          </li>
          <li>
            The target must also have a <var class="field">[[Call]]</var> internal method.
          </li>
        </ul>

a2:T9886,<p>Well-known intrinsics are built-in objects that are explicitly referenced by the algorithms of this specification and which usually have <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm">realm</a></emu-xref>-specific identities. Unless otherwise specified each intrinsic object actually corresponds to a set of similar objects, one per <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm">realm</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <p>Within this specification a reference such as <emu-intrinsic>%name%</emu-intrinsic> means the intrinsic object, associated with the current <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm">realm</a></emu-xref>, corresponding to the name. A reference such as <emu-intrinsic>%name.a.b%</emu-intrinsic> means, as if the <emu-val>"b"</emu-val> property of the value of the <emu-val>"a"</emu-val> property of the intrinsic object <emu-intrinsic>%name%</emu-intrinsic> was accessed prior to any ECMAScript code being evaluated. Determination of the current <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#realm">realm</a></emu-xref> and its intrinsics is described in <emu-xref href="#sec-execution-contexts"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-execution-contexts">9.4</a></emu-xref>. The well-known intrinsics are listed in <emu-xref href="#table-well-known-intrinsic-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-well-known-intrinsic-objects">Table 6</a></emu-xref>.</p>
        <emu-table id="table-well-known-intrinsic-objects" caption="Well-Known Intrinsic Objects" oldids="table-7" data-num="6">
          <table>
            <thead>
              <tr>
                <th>

                  ECMAScript Language Association
                </th>
              </tr>
            </thead>
            <tr>
              <td>
                <emu-intrinsic>%AggregateError%</emu-intrinsic>
              </td>
              <td>
                <emu-val>"AggregateError"</emu-val>
              </td>
              <td>
                The <code>AggregateError</code> <emu-not-ref>constructor</emu-not-ref> (<emu-xref href="#sec-aggregate-error-constructor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/fundamental-objects#sec-aggregate-error-constructor">20.5.7.1</a></emu-xref>)
              </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>

              </td>

              </td>

              </td>

      69:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-well-known-intrinsic-objects","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"6174-well-known-intrinsic-objects","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.1.7.4"}]," Well-Known Intrinsic Objects"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"6174-well-known-intrinsic-objects"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$a2"}}]]}]
a3:Tf28,<p>The <dfn variants="Lists">List</dfn> type is used to explain the evaluation of argument lists (see <emu-xref href="#sec-argument-lists"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#sec-argument-lists">13.3.8</a></emu-xref>) in <code>new</code> expressions, in function calls, and in other algorithms where a simple ordered list of values is needed. Values of the List type are simply ordered sequences of list elements containing the individual values. These sequences may be of any length. The elements of a list may be randomly accessed using 0-origin indices. For notational convenience an array-like syntax can be used to access List elements. For example, <var>args</var>[2] is shorthand for saying the 3<sup>rd</sup> element of the List <var>args</var>.</p>
      <p>When an algorithm iterates over the elements of a List without specifying an order, the order used is the order of the elements in the List.</p>
      <p>For notational convenience within this specification, a literal syntax can be used to express a new List value. For example, « 1, 2 » defines a List value that has two elements each of which is initialized to a specific value. A new empty List can be expressed as « ».</p>
      <p>In this specification, the phrase “the <dfn id="list-concatenation">list-concatenation</dfn> of <var>A</var>, <var>B</var>, ...” (where each argument is a possibly empty List) denotes a new List value whose elements are the concatenation of the elements (in order) of each of the arguments (in order).</p>
      <p>As applied to a List of Strings, the phrase “sorted according to <dfn id="lexicographic-code-unit-order">lexicographic code unit order</dfn>” means sorting by the numeric value of each code unit up to the length of the shorter string, and sorting the shorter string before the longer string if all are equal, as described in the abstract operation <emu-xref aoid="IsLessThan"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-islessthan">IsLessThan</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <p>The <dfn variants="Records">Record</dfn> type is used to describe data aggregations within the algorithms of this specification. A Record type value consists of one or more named fields. The value of each field is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref> or specification value. Field names are always enclosed in double brackets, for example <var class="field">[[Value]]</var>.</p>
      <p>For notational convenience within this specification, an object literal-like syntax can be used to express a Record value. For example, { <var class="field">[[Field1]]</var>: 42, <var class="field">[[Field2]]</var>: <emu-val>false</emu-val>, <var class="field">[[Field3]]</var>: <emu-const>empty</emu-const> } defines a Record value that has three fields, each of which is initialized to a specific value. Field name order is not significant. Any fields that are not explicitly listed are considered to be absent.</p>
      <p>In specification text and algorithms, dot notation may be used to refer to a specific field of a Record value. For example, if R is the record shown in the previous paragraph then R.<var class="field">[[Field2]]</var> is shorthand for “the field of R named <var class="field">[[Field2]]</var>”.</p>
      <p>Schema for commonly used Record field combinations may be named, and that name may be used as a prefix to a literal Record value to identify the specific kind of aggregations that is being described. For example: PropertyDescriptor { <var class="field">[[Value]]</var>: 42, <var class="field">[[Writable]]</var>: <emu-val>false</emu-val>, <var class="field">[[Configurable]]</var>: <emu-val>true</emu-val> }.</p>
    6b:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-list-and-record-specification-type","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"622-the-list-and-record-specification-types","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.2"}]," The List and Record Specification Types"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"622-the-list-and-record-specification-types"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$a3"}}]]}]
a4:Tdce,<p>The <em>Set</em> type is used to explain a collection of unordered elements for use in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref>. It is distinct from the ECMAScript collection type of the same name. To disambiguate, instances of the ECMAScript collection are consistently referred to as “<emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/keyed-collections#sec-set-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/keyed-collections#sec-set-objects">Set objects</a></emu-xref>” within this specification. Values of the Set type are simple collections of elements, where no element appears more than once. Elements may be added to and removed from Sets. Sets may be unioned, intersected, or subtracted from each other.</p>
      <p>The <dfn variants="Relations">Relation</dfn> type is used to explain constraints on Sets. Values of the Relation type are Sets of ordered pairs of values from its value domain. For example, a Relation on <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">Memory events</a></emu-xref> is a set of ordered pairs of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">Memory events</a></emu-xref>. For a Relation <var>R</var> and two values <var>a</var> and <var>b</var> in the value domain of <var>R</var>, <var>a</var> <var>R</var> <var>b</var> is shorthand for saying the ordered pair (<var>a</var>, <var>b</var>) is a member of <var>R</var>. A Relation is the <dfn id="least-relation">least Relation</dfn> with respect to some conditions when it is the smallest Relation that satisfies those conditions.</p>
      <p>A <dfn variants="strict partial orders">strict partial order</dfn> is a Relation value <var>R</var> that satisfies the following.</p>
      <ul>
        <li>
          <p>For all <var>a</var>, <var>b</var>, and <var>c</var> in <var>R</var>'s domain:</p>
          <ul>
            <li>It is not the case that <var>a</var> <var>R</var> <var>a</var>, and</li>
            <li>If <var>a</var> <var>R</var> <var>b</var> and <var>b</var> <var>R</var> <var>c</var>, then <var>a</var> <var>R</var> <var>c</var>.</li>
          </ul>
        </li>
      </ul>
      <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The two properties above are called irreflexivity and transitivity, respectively.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <p>A <dfn variants="strict total orders">strict total order</dfn> is a Relation value <var>R</var> that satisfies the following.</p>
      <ul>
        <li>
          <p>For all <var>a</var>, <var>b</var>, and <var>c</var> in <var>R</var>'s domain:</p>
          <ul>
            <li><var>a</var> is <var>b</var> or <var>a</var> <var>R</var> <var>b</var> or <var>b</var> <var>R</var> <var>a</var>, and</li>
            <li>It is not the case that <var>a</var> <var>R</var> <var>a</var>, and</li>
            <li>If <var>a</var> <var>R</var> <var>b</var> and <var>b</var> <var>R</var> <var>c</var>, then <var>a</var> <var>R</var> <var>c</var>.</li>
          </ul>
        </li>
      </ul>
      <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The three properties above are called totality, irreflexivity, and transitivity, respectively.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
    6c:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-set-and-relation-specification-type","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"623-the-set-and-relation-specification-types","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.3"}]," The Set and Relation Specification Types"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"623-the-set-and-relation-specification-types"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$a4"}}]]}]
a5:T13d3,<p>The <dfn variants="Completion Records">Completion Record</dfn> specification type is used to explain the runtime propagation of values and control flow such as the behaviour of statements (<code>break</code>, <code>continue</code>, <code>return</code> and <code>throw</code>) that perform nonlocal transfers of control.</p>
      <p><emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Records have the fields defined in <emu-xref href="#table-completion-record-fields"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-completion-record-fields">Table 7</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <emu-table id="table-completion-record-fields" caption="Completion Record Fields" oldids="table-8" data-num="7">
        <table>
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>
                Field Name
              </th>
              <th>
                Value
              </th>
              <th>
                Meaning
              </th>
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tr>
            <td>
              <var class="field">[[Type]]</var>
            </td>
            <td>
              <emu-const>normal</emu-const>, <emu-const>break</emu-const>, <emu-const>continue</emu-const>, <emu-const>return</emu-const>, or <emu-const>throw</emu-const>
            </td>
            <td>
              The type of completion that occurred.
            </td>
          </tr>
          <tr>
            <td>
              <var class="field">[[Value]]</var>
            </td>
            <td>
              any value except a <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Record
            </td>
            <td>
              The value that was produced.
            </td>
          </tr>
          <tr>
            <td>
              <var class="field">[[Target]]</var>
            </td>
            <td>
              a String or <emu-const>empty</emu-const>
            </td>
            <td>
              The target label for directed control transfers.
            </td>
          </tr>
        </table>
      </emu-table>
      <p>The following shorthand terms are sometimes used to refer to <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Records.</p>
      <ul>
        <li><dfn variants="normal completions">normal completion</dfn> refers to any <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Record with a <var class="field">[[Type]]</var> value of <emu-const>normal</emu-const>.</li>
        <li><dfn variants="break completions">break completion</dfn> refers to any <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Record with a <var class="field">[[Type]]</var> value of <emu-const>break</emu-const>.</li>
        <li><dfn variants="continue completions">continue completion</dfn> refers to any <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Record with a <var class="field">[[Type]]</var> value of <emu-const>continue</emu-const>.</li>
        <li><dfn variants="return completions">return completion</dfn> refers to any <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Record with a <var class="field">[[Type]]</var> value of <emu-const>return</emu-const>.</li>
        <li><dfn variants="throw completions">throw completion</dfn> refers to any <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Record with a <var class="field">[[Type]]</var> value of <emu-const>throw</emu-const>.</li>
        <li><dfn variants="abrupt completions">abrupt completion</dfn> refers to any <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Record with a <var class="field">[[Type]]</var> value other than <emu-const>normal</emu-const>.</li>
        <li>a <dfn variants="normal completions containing">normal completion containing</dfn> some type of value refers to a normal completion that has a value of that type in its <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> field.</li>
      </ul>
      <p>Callable objects that are defined in this specification only return a normal completion or a throw completion. Returning any other kind of <emu-xref aoid="Completion"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-completion-ao">Completion</a></emu-xref> Record is considered an editorial error.</p>
      <p><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">Implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> callable objects must return either a normal completion or a throw completion.</p>

      6d:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-completion-record-specification-type","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"624-the-completion-record-specification-type","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.4"}]," The Completion Record Specification Type"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"624-the-completion-record-specification-type"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$a5"}}],"$La6","$La7","$La8","$La9"]}]
aa:T16a7,<p>The <dfn variants="Reference Records">Reference Record</dfn> type is used to explain the behaviour of such operators as <code>delete</code>, <code>typeof</code>, the assignment operators, the <code>super</code> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#sec-keywords-and-reserved-words"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#sec-keywords-and-reserved-words">keyword</a></emu-xref> and other language features. For example, the left-hand operand of an assignment is expected to produce a Reference Record.</p>
      <p>A Reference Record is a resolved name or (possibly not-yet-resolved) property binding; its fields are defined by <emu-xref href="#table-reference-record-fields"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-reference-record-fields">Table 8</a></emu-xref>.</p>

      <emu-table id="table-reference-record-fields" caption="Reference Record Fields" data-num="8">
        <table>
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>Field Name</th>
              <th>Value</th>
              <th>Meaning</th>
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tr>
            <td oldids="sec-getbase,ao-getbase"><var class="field">[[Base]]</var></td>
            <td>an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref>, an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records">Environment Record</a></emu-xref>, or <emu-const>unresolvable</emu-const></td>
            <td>The value or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records">Environment Record</a></emu-xref> which holds the binding.           </tr>
          <tr>
            <td><var class="field">[[ThisValue]]</var></td>

            <td>If not <emu-const>empty</emu-const>, the Reference Record represents a property binding that was expressed using the <code>super</code> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#sec-keywords-and-reserved-words"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#sec-keywords-and-reserved-words">keyword</a></emu-xref>; it is called a <dfn id="super-reference-record" oldids="super-reference" variants="Super Reference Records">Super Reference Record</dfn> and its <var class="field">[[Base]]</var> value will never be an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records">Environment Record</a></emu-xref>. In that case, the <var class="field">[[ThisValue]]</var> field holds the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value at the time the Reference Record was created.</td>

      <p>The following <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> are used in this specification to operate upon Reference Records:</p>

      6e:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-reference-record-specification-type","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"625-the-reference-record-specification-type","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.5"}]," The Reference Record Specification Type"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"625-the-reference-record-specification-type"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$aa"}}],"$Lab","$Lac","$Lad","$Lae","$Laf","$Lb0","$Lb1","$Lb2","$Lb3"]}]
b4:T930,<p>The <dfn variants="Property Descriptors">Property Descriptor</dfn> type is used to explain the manipulation and reification of Object property attributes. A Property Descriptor is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Record</a></emu-xref> with zero or more fields, where each field's name is an attribute name and its value is a corresponding attribute value as specified in <emu-xref href="#sec-property-attributes"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-attributes">6.1.7.1</a></emu-xref>. The schema name used within this specification to tag literal descriptions of Property Descriptor records is “PropertyDescriptor”.</p>
      <p>Property Descriptor values may be further classified as data Property Descriptors and accessor Property Descriptors based upon the existence or use of certain fields. A data Property Descriptor is one that includes any fields named either <var class="field">[[Value]]</var> or <var class="field">[[Writable]]</var>. An accessor Property Descriptor is one that includes any fields named either <var class="field">[[Get]]</var> or <var class="field">[[Set]]</var>. Any Property Descriptor may have fields named <var class="field">[[Enumerable]]</var> and <var class="field">[[Configurable]]</var>. A Property Descriptor value may not be both a data Property Descriptor and an accessor Property Descriptor; however, it may be neither (in which case it is a generic Property Descriptor). A <dfn>fully populated Property Descriptor</dfn> is one that is either an accessor Property Descriptor or a data Property Descriptor and that has all of the corresponding fields defined in <emu-xref href="#table-object-property-attributes"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-object-property-attributes">Table 3</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <p>The following <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> are used in this specification to operate upon Property Descriptor values:</p>

      6f:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-property-descriptor-specification-type","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"626-the-property-descriptor-specification-type","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.6"}]," The Property Descriptor Specification Type"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"626-the-property-descriptor-specification-type"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$b4"}}],["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-isaccessordescriptor","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"6261-isaccessordescriptor--propertydesc-","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.6.1"}]," IsAccessorDescriptor ( ",["$","var",null,{"children":"propertyDesc"}]," )"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"6261-isaccessordescriptor--propertydesc-"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"<p>The abstract operation IsAccessorDescriptor takes argument <var>propertyDesc</var> (a <emu-xref href=\"/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type\"><a href=\"/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type\">Property Descriptor</a></emu-xref>) and returns a Boolean. It performs the following steps when called:</p>\n        <emu-alg><ol><li class=\"exit\">If <var>propertyDesc</var> has a <var class=\"field\">[[Get]]</var> field, return <emu-val>true</emu-val>.</li><li class=\"exit\">If <var>propertyDesc</var> has a <var class=\"field\">[[Set]]</var> field, return <emu-val>true</emu-val>.</li><li class=\"exit\">Return <emu-val>false</emu-val>.</li></ol></emu-alg>\n      "}}]]}],"$Lb5","$Lb6","$Lb7","$Lb8","$Lb9"]}]
70:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-lexical-environment-and-environment-record-specification-types","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"627-the-environment-record-specification-type","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.7"}]," The Environment Record Specification Type"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"627-the-environment-record-specification-type"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"<p>The <emu-xref href=\"/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records\"><a href=\"/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records\">Environment Record</a></emu-xref> type is used to explain the behaviour of name resolution in nested functions and blocks. This type and the operations upon it are defined in <emu-xref href=\"#sec-environment-records\"><a href=\"/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records\">9.1</a></emu-xref>.</p>\n    "}}]]}]
ba:Tae2,<p>The <dfn variants="Abstract Closures">Abstract Closure</dfn> specification type is used to refer to algorithm steps together with a collection of values. Abstract Closures are meta-values and are invoked using function application style such as <var>closure</var>(<var>arg1</var>, <var>arg2</var>). Like <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref>, invocations perform the algorithm steps described by the Abstract Closure.</p>
      <p>In algorithm steps that create an Abstract Closure, values are captured with the verb “capture” followed by a list of aliases. When an Abstract Closure is created, it captures the value that is associated with each alias at that time. In steps that specify the algorithm to be performed when an Abstract Closure is called, each captured value is referred to by the alias that was used to capture the value.</p>
      <p>If an Abstract Closure returns a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref>, that <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref> must be either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion</a></emu-xref> or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">throw completion</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <p>Abstract Closures are created inline as part of other algorithms, shown in the following example.</p>
      <emu-alg example><ol><li>Let <var>addend</var> be 41.</li><li>Let <var>closure</var> be a new Abstract Closure with parameters (<var>x</var>) that captures <var>addend</var> and performs the following steps when called:<ol><li class="exit">Return <var>x</var> + <var>addend</var>.</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>value</var> be <var>closure</var>(1).</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>value</var> is 42.</li></ol></emu-alg>
    71:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-abstract-closure","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"628-the-abstract-closure-specification-type","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.8"}]," The Abstract Closure Specification Type"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"628-the-abstract-closure-specification-type"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$ba"}}]]}]
bb:T11ff,<p>The <dfn variants="Data Blocks">Data Block</dfn> specification type is used to describe a distinct and mutable sequence of byte-sized (8 bit) numeric values. A <dfn variants="byte values">byte value</dfn> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 0 to 255. A Data Block value is created with a fixed number of bytes that each have the initial value 0.</p>
      <p>For notational convenience within this specification, an array-like syntax can be used to access the individual bytes of a Data Block value. This notation presents a Data Block value as a 0-based <emu-not-ref>integer-indexed</emu-not-ref> sequence of bytes. For example, if <var>db</var> is a 5 byte Data Block value then <var>db</var>[2] can be used to access its 3<sup>rd</sup> byte.</p>
      <p>A data block that resides in memory that can be referenced from multiple <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#agent">agents</a></emu-xref> concurrently is designated a <dfn variants="Shared Data Blocks">Shared Data Block</dfn>. A Shared Data Block has an identity (for the purposes of equality testing Shared Data Block values) that is <em>address-free</em>: it is tied not to the virtual addresses the block is mapped to in any process, but to the set of locations in memory that the block represents. Two data blocks are equal only if the sets of the locations they contain are equal; otherwise, they are not equal and the intersection of the sets of locations they contain is empty. Finally, Shared Data Blocks can be distinguished from Data Blocks.</p>
      <p>The semantics of Shared Data Blocks is defined using <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">Shared Data Block events</a></emu-xref> by the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref>. <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">Abstract operations</a></emu-xref> below introduce <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">Shared Data Block events</a></emu-xref> and act as the interface between evaluation semantics and the event semantics of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref>. The events form a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-candidate-executions"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-candidate-executions">candidate execution</a></emu-xref>, on which the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref> acts as a filter. Please consult the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref> for full semantics.</p>
      <p><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model-fundamentals">Shared Data Block events</a></emu-xref> are modelled by <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Records</a></emu-xref>, defined in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/memory-model#sec-memory-model">memory model</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <p>The following <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> are used in this specification to operate upon Data Block values:</p>

      72:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-data-blocks","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"629-data-blocks","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"6.2.9"}]," Data Blocks"],["$","$L51",null,{"id":"629-data-blocks"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$bb"}}],"$Lbc","$Lbd","$Lbe"]}]
bf:T143b,<p>The PrivateElement type is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Record</a></emu-xref> used in the specification of private class fields, methods, and accessors. Although <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-property-descriptor-specification-type">Property Descriptors</a></emu-xref> are not used for private elements, private fields behave similarly to non-configurable, non-enumerable, writable <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data properties</a></emu-xref>, private methods behave similarly to non-configurable, non-enumerable, non-writable <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">data properties</a></emu-xref>, and private accessors behave similarly to non-configurable, non-enumerable <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">accessor properties</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      <p>Values of the PrivateElement type are <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">Record</a></emu-xref> values whose fields are defined by <emu-xref href="#table-privateelement-fields"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#table-privateelement-fields">Table 9</a></emu-xref>. Such values are referred to as <dfn variants="PrivateElement">PrivateElements</dfn>.</p>

c4:Tb13,<p>The abstract operation Number::multiply takes arguments <var>x</var> (a Number) and <var>y</var> (a Number) and returns a Number. It performs multiplication according to the rules of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography">IEEE 754-2019</a></emu-xref> binary double-precision arithmetic, producing the product of <var>x</var> and <var>y</var>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
          <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">If <var>x</var> is <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> or <var>y</var> is <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>, return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</li><li>If <var>x</var> is either <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, then<ol><li class="exit">If <var>y</var> is either <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>y</var> > <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <var>x</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return -<var>x</var>.</li></ol></li><li>If <var>y</var> is either <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, then<ol><li class="exit">If <var>x</var> is either <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>x</var> > <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <var>y</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return -<var>y</var>.</li></ol></li><li>If <var>x</var> is <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, then<ol><li class="exit">If <var>y</var> is <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <var>y</var> &lt; <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li></ol></li><li>If <var>y</var> is <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, then<ol><li class="exit">If <var>x</var> &lt; <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li></ol></li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>x</var>) × <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>y</var>)).</li></ol></emu-alg>
          <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">

c6:Tfa4,<p>The abstract operation Number::remainder takes arguments <var>numerator</var> (a Number) and <var>denominator</var> (a Number) and returns a Number. It yields the remainder from an implied division of its operands where <var>numerator</var> is the dividend and <var>denominator</var> is the divisor. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
          <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">If <var>numerator</var> is <emu-val>NaN</emu-val> or <var>denominator</var> is <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>, return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>numerator</var> is either <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>denominator</var> is either <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <var>numerator</var>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>denominator</var> is either <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>numerator</var> is either <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <var>numerator</var>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>numerator</var> and <var>denominator</var> are <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#finite">finite</a></emu-xref> and non-zero.</li><li>Let <var>quotient</var> be <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>numerator</var>) / <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>denominator</var>).</li><li>Let <var>truncatedQuotient</var> be <emu-xref aoid="truncate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#eqn-truncate">truncate</a></emu-xref>(<var>quotient</var>).</li><li>Let <var>remainder</var> be <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>numerator</var>) - (<emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>denominator</var>) × <var>truncatedQuotient</var>).</li><li class="exit">If <var>remainder</var> = 0 and <var>numerator</var> &lt; <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>remainder</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
          <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>In C and C++, the remainder operator accepts only integral operands; in ECMAScript, it also accepts floating-point operands.</p>
          </div></emu-note>
          <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">The result of a floating-point remainder operation as computed by the <code>%</code> operator is not the same as the “remainder” operation defined by <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography">IEEE 754-2019</a></emu-xref>. The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/bibliography#sec-bibliography">IEEE 754-2019</a></emu-xref> “remainder” operation computes the remainder from a rounding division, not a truncating division, and so its behaviour is not analogous to that of the usual <emu-not-ref>integer</emu-not-ref> remainder operator. Instead the ECMAScript language defines <code>%</code> on floating-point operations to behave in a manner analogous to that of the Java <emu-not-ref>integer</emu-not-ref> remainder operator; this may be compared with the C library function fmod.</div></emu-note>

cd:T2950,<p>The abstract operation Number::toString takes arguments <var>x</var> (a Number) and <var>radix</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> in the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#inclusive-interval">inclusive interval</a></emu-xref> from 2 to 36) and returns a String. It represents <var>x</var> as a String using a positional numeral system with radix <var>radix</var>. The digits used in the representation of a number using radix <var>r</var> are taken from the first <var>r</var> code units of <emu-val>"0123456789abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz"</emu-val> in order. The representation of numbers with magnitude greater than or equal to <emu-val>1</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> never includes leading zeroes. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
          <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">If <var>x</var> is <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>, return <emu-val>"NaN"</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>x</var> is either <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>"0"</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>x</var> &lt; <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#string-concatenation">string-concatenation</a></emu-xref> of <emu-val>"-"</emu-val> and Number::toString(-<var>x</var>, <var>radix</var>).</li><li class="exit">If <var>x</var> is <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>"Infinity"</emu-val>.</li><li id="step-number-tostring-intermediate-values">Let <var>n</var>, <var>k</var>, and <var>s</var> be <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref> such that <var>k</var> ≥ 1, <var>radix</var><sup><var>k</var> - 1</sup> ≤ <var>s</var> &lt; <var>radix</var><sup><var>k</var></sup>, <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>s</var> × <var>radix</var><sup><var>n</var> - <var>k</var></sup>) is <var>x</var>, and <var>k</var> is as small as possible. Note that <var>k</var> is the number of digits in the representation of <var>s</var> using radix <var>radix</var>, that <var>s</var> is not divisible by <var>radix</var>, and that the least significant digit of <var>s</var> is not necessarily uniquely determined by these criteria.</li><li>If <var>radix</var> ≠ 10 or <var>n</var> is in the <emu-xref <var>s</var> using radix <var>radix</var></li><li><var>n</var> - <var>k</var> occurrences of the code unit 0x0030 (DIGIT ZERO)</li></ul></li></ol></li><li>If <var>n</var> > 0, then<ol><li           <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>The following observations may be useful as guidelines for implementations, but are not part of the normative requirements of this Standard:</p>
            <ul>
              <li>
                If x is any Number value other than <emu-val>-0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, then <emu-xref aoid="ToNumber"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tonumber">ToNumber</a></emu-xref>(<emu-xref aoid="ToString"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tostring">ToString</a></emu-xref>(x)) is x.
              </li>
              <li>
                The least significant digit of s is not always uniquely determined by the requirements listed in step <emu-xref href="#step-number-tostring-intermediate-values"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#step-number-tostring-intermediate-values">5</a></emu-xref>.
              </li>
            </ul>
          </div></emu-note>
          <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>For implementations that provide more accurate conversions than required by the rules above, it is recommended that the following alternative version of step <emu-xref href="#step-number-tostring-intermediate-values"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#step-number-tostring-intermediate-values">5</a></emu-xref> be used as a guideline:</p>
            <emu-alg replaces-step="step-number-tostring-intermediate-values"><ol><li>Let <var>n</var>, <var>k</var>, and <var>s</var> be <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integers</a></emu-xref> such that <var>k</var> ≥ 1, <var>radix</var><sup><var>k</var> - 1</sup> ≤ <var>s</var> &lt; <var>radix</var><sup><var>k</var></sup>, <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>s</var> × <var>radix</var><sup><var>n</var> - <var>k</var></sup>) is <var>x</var>, and <var>k</var> is as small as possible. If there are multiple possibilities for <var>s</var>, choose <var>s</var> such that <var>s</var> × <var>radix</var><sup><var>n</var> - <var>k</var></sup> is closest to <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>x</var>). If there are two such possible values of <var>s</var>, choose the one that is even. Note that <var>k</var> is the number of digits in the representation of <var>s</var> using radix <var>radix</var> and that <var>s</var> is not divisible by <var>radix</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
          </div></emu-note>
          <emu-note data-num="3"><span class="note">Note 3</span><div class="note-contents">
            <p>Implementers of ECMAScript may find useful the paper and code written by David M. Gay for binary-to-decimal conversion of floating-point numbers:</p>
            <p>
              Gay, David M. Correctly Rounded Binary-Decimal and Decimal-Binary Conversions. Numerical Analysis, Manuscript 90-10. AT&amp;T Bell Laboratories (Murray Hill, New Jersey). 30 November 1990. Available as<br>
              <a href="https://ampl.com/_archive/first-website/REFS/rounding.pdf">https://ampl.com/_archive/first-website/REFS/rounding.pdf</a>. Associated code available as<br>
              <a href="http://netlib.sandia.gov/fp/dtoa.c">http://netlib.sandia.gov/fp/dtoa.c</a> and as<br>
              <a href="http://netlib.sandia.gov/fp/g_fmt.c">http://netlib.sandia.gov/fp/g_fmt.c</a> and may also be found at the various <code>netlib</code> mirror sites.
            </p>
          </div></emu-note>
